(needs_source: no answer is recorded for Umm Rayyan) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:38:00}
- He sums up what the callers said: an appeal to municipalities and official bodies to check signs, since our language is our identity, Na'ima's point that the matter lies in schools and the media as well, and that father and mother should not speak different languages before the children except by necessity and that we should alert a son or daughter who uses foreign jargon at home. This is the lecturer's summary. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:41:00}
- He adds corrections: say 'nasrani' not 'masihi' because God named them Nasara, and holds that a Muslim's Islam is not complete without belief in [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Isa]] and the Gospel sent down to him, that 'hubut' is not 'nuzul' ([[passage/quran-2-61|al-Baqara 61]]), that 'nasa'im' is the plural of 'nasim', that 'wasita' is the jewel in a necklace and 'wasata' is mediation to reconcile, that 'mamnun' is not 'shakir', and that the plural is 'al-mudirun' not 'mudara'. These are the lecturer's linguistic views; what touches religion is his opinion. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:44:30}

## Episode 27: the character of muru'a in Islam
- He wonders at the small fruit of lessons of knowledge, sermons and satellite channels, and sees the cause in behavioural handicaps: the arrogant, the vain, the show-off, the liar and the two-faced who meets you smiling then slanders you. He mentions that [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]] saw [[person/musa|Musa]] as a corrupter in the land ([[passage/quran-40-26|Ghafir 26]]) and that the people of [[person/lut|Lut]] said of Lut and his household that they are people who keep themselves pure ([[passage/quran-27-56|al-Naml 56]]), so the corrupt person throws his faults on you, which the psychologists call projection. This is the lecturer's interpretation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:03:00}
- He says the soul has three callers: one to the character of Satan, one to the conduct of the beast and one to the character of the angel. One in whom pride, envy, hatred, rancour, harm and wrong are joined is a human devil, and the human devil takes precedence over the jinn devil because one seeks refuge in God from the jinn devil, whereas the human devil may be from kin, a child, a father or a mother. He mentions the proud and vain who says I am the candle that burns to light others. This is the lecturer's classification. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:05:00}
- He calls on a person not to count himself as having favour over people but to thank them for leaving him room to do good. He tells that [[person/aisha|Aisha]], when a poor man carried her charity and said God reward you, would say rather may God reward you, since he carried her provision to the hereafter. He tells (in sense) the report of the sheep that was distributed with only the foreleg left, and the Prophet said rather all of it remained and the foreleg went, and that a person has of his wealth only what he ate and used up, wore and wore out, or gave in charity and so kept. These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: reports and stories given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:07:00}
- He explains 'a soul will know what it sent ahead and what it left behind' ([[passage/quran-82-5|al-Infitar 5]]): what a person sent ahead is his prayer, alms, pilgrimage and fasting, and what he left behind is a righteous child who prays for him and continuing charity ([[concept/sadaqa-jariya|continuing charity]]) such as building a mosque, a copy of the Qur'an, digging a well, and an endowment for orphans, widows and students of knowledge. He ascribes to our scholars that one who does not pray for his father and mother after every prayer, living or dead, has been disobedient to them (his claim). He says the believer's traces remain in the place of his prostration, and that when the wicked die people are relieved of them (in sense). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the ruling on praying for parents ascribed to scholars and the reports on traces have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:10:00}
- He observes that people say the former minister and the earlier official but do not say Abu Bakr formerly, nor Abu Hanifa, Malik or Ibn Taymiyya, but say Abu Hanifa says in the present tense though he died in 150 of the Hijra, so the people of knowledge live on in their memory, and says those who lived for religion, God has not let their memory die, and how many millions pray for Abu Bakr and Umar. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] and [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]. This is the lecturer's observation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:12:00}
- He warns of 'raids', the first being the raid of the angel of death, and says some people have a defect in belief: they say God provides and then say so-and-so cut off my provision, whereas the believer does not say that because only God cuts provision, through death, and the martyrs' provision is not cut off ([[passage/quran-9-111|al-Tawba 111]]). This is the lecturer's view on belief. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:14:00}
- He takes up the character of muru'a (manly virtue): when it evaporates from homes scowling faces appear, and whoever follows the caller of Satan or the beast comes to hate people and be hated. He gives the image of the bee that settles only on flowers, mentions the saying of [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]], be among people like a tree they throw stones at and it throws them fruit (without a chain), and cites the saying to repel evil with what is better so that the one between you and whom there was enmity becomes a close friend ([[passage/quran-41-34|Fussilat 34]]) and what follows ([[passage/quran-41-35|35]] and [[passage/quran-41-36|36]]) on seeking refuge from Satan's prompting. He says doing good is not for the good-doer alone, and that keeping kinship is not by returning like for like (given in sense). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the saying of al-Hasan al-Basri and the saying on keeping kinship have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:17:00}
- He says that keeping ties is not only to visit those who visit you but to keep ties with the one who cut you off, and that some spouses and children encourage evil and say do not visit your proud uncle, while He has said that among your spouses and children are enemies to you so beware of them ([[passage/quran-64-14|al-Taghabun 14]]), and counts the husband with no protective jealousy as one not trusted with what is sacred. He tells that a man asked for a slave girl of [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]] in marriage and was told she has a fault, that she looks at men, and the suitor said what fault is that, so Ibn Abbas refused him. This is the lecturer's view and story. (needs_source: the story of Ibn Abbas and the suitor has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:19:00}
- He tells the account of the Prophet's proposal to [[person/aisha|Aisha]]: a woman came to Abu Bakr and did not find him, and told [[person/umm-ruman|Umm Ruman]], who said wait until Abu Bakr comes; he had promised her to the son of al-Mut'im ibn Adi, so he went to al-Mut'im, whose wife said we fear you will turn our son from his fathers' religion, and Abu Bakr said to al-Mut'im I am speaking to you, what has it to do with you, whereupon the promise was lifted and the Prophet proposed and married her. He infers that guardianship is not by force and abuse but by embracing and example, and that the absence of the real role of man and woman is what breaks homes apart. This is the lecturer's story and view. (needs_source: the account of the proposal to Aisha is given in his telling without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:22:00}
- He returns to the caller of the angel: the angel is good-doing, dutiful and obedient, nothing leaves his tongue but good, glorification is to him as breathing is to us, and he neither eats, drinks nor sleeps. He mentions that the Prophet was a human being ([[passage/quran-18-110|al-Kahf 110]]) who is pleased and angry, sleeps, eats and marries. Then he says an institution without love between its head and its workers is like graves faced with marble outside and worms within, and that favouring the applauders and shunning those who advise is not the muru'a of a Muslim. These are the lecturer's views. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:25:00}
- He takes up the muru'a of the limbs, first the tongue with good words. He tells (in sense) that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] said he loved the world only for three things, among them sitting with people who pick the best speech as the best fruit is picked. He says son of Adam, speak good and profit or be silent and be safe, that a tongue's wound is worse than the spear's, and that you own a word while you have not uttered it and once uttered it owns you. He repeats that the angel moves two miles from the liar for the stench of what left his mouth, that God veils sins and they have no smell, and that reckoning is in the hands of the Forbearing. These are the lecturer's view and stories. (needs_source: reports and stories given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:28:00}
- He says the muru'a of character is to be just with those you love and those you do not ([[passage/quran-5-8|al-Ma'ida 8]]), to accept the truth from whoever brings it, to look at what was said and not at who said it, and that wisdom is the believer's lost property (in sense). He says the Arab by nature is emotional and quick to feeling and finds fault with people and time but not with himself. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. (needs_source: the report on wisdom as the believer's lost property is in sense without a chain and the generalisation on the Arab has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:31:00}
- He gives the example of one who places a magnifying lens on people's faults and a reducing lens on his own, so he sees his brother's fault as a kilometre and his own as a millimetre, and here the tree of pride grows, and mentions the employee who thinks the institution rests on him. He tells (in sense) that when God loves a servant He calls [[person/jibril|Jibril]] to love him, who calls among the angels, and acceptance is laid down for him on earth. This is the lecturer's illustration. (needs_source: the report on Jibril's love is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:33:00}
- He tells that [[person/ali-zayn-al-abidin|Ali Zayn al-Abidin]] was at the circumambulation by the time of Hisham ibn Abd al-Malik and the people made way for him, and that a man who did not know him insulted him and called him showy, wicked and hypocritical, and he answered God reward you with good, then said you reminded me of three qualities and I have more of them, would you like me to list them, and the man said I testify that you are the son of the Messenger of God. He mentions that [[person/salman-al-farisi|Salman al-Farisi]] used to say I am the son of Islam, and that a young man in the time of [[person/al-hajjaj-ibn-yusuf|al-Hajjaj]] said I am the son of the man before whom God made the angels prostrate ([[person/adam|Adam]]). These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: reports and stories given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:00}
- He complains that the boy cannot write two lines without errors of language and grammar, and says homes have not become safe because we did not secure our language in them, and cites verses of [[person/hafiz-ibrahim|Hafiz Ibrahim]] in the voice of the Arabic language, which are not reproduced here. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:00}
- He takes up the muru'a of wealth: that it be earned lawfully, spent lawfully and used to be generous to people, while the miser hates himself, his wife hates him, his children tire of him and he lives the life of the poor and is reckoned as the rich. He relates that [[person/yaqub|Yaqub]] with the shirt of [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] wanted to borrow a needle from a miser to stitch it (a story without a chain). He mentions the muru'a of standing: that a person spend his standing on good intercession, for as wealth has alms so do standing, knowledge and office. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the story of Yaqub and the needle has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:38:00}
- He mentions that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] on cold days sat in his house with only what covers his nakedness to share the poor's cold, and that [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] used to say he was fasting when food was served to him while he was over Egypt's treasuries in the famine, for fear he would forget the hungry (two stories without chains). He criticises insistence on stockpiling food in freezers as if a famine were coming, and calls for planning while leaving the matter to God ([[passage/quran-96-6|al-Alaq 6]] and [[passage/quran-96-7|7]]). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the stories of Umar and Yusuf have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:41:00}
- He mentions the muru'a of leaving: leaving quarrels, since some brothers spend ten or twenty years in the courts over a small plot and spend on lawyers more than its price, leaving harsh blame, since the Arabs are good at attack and not at gentle reproach, and leaving pressing in demands, like asking for a glass of tea twenty times. He ends with the prayer that the character of muru'a return to homes so that souls grow calm. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:43:00}

## Episode 28: noses in the home and the rights of mother and wife
- He says the episode may be thorny because it concerns the noses put into homes from outside, especially by the husband's mother or the wife's mother in new homes, and the mixing of rights and duties in the husband between his mother and his wife. He holds that the woman's role in civilisation is stronger and loftier than the man's, since man developed material civilisation and the righteous woman preserved the civilisation of values by raising the children. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:03:00}
- He asks why happiness evaporated from our homes despite the washing machine, the vacuum cleaner and the oven, complaining that women suffer gout, brittle bones and back pain while the grandparents' homes were better off with few means, and says the husband's mother was closer to her son's wife than to her son and defended her against him (his claim). He holds that God's Book has not changed and rights are still clear, but the concept of happiness has changed, and we look at the symptom and do not seek the disease. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the comparison between grandparents' homes and today's and the claim about the husband's mother have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:07:00}
- He says the marriage contract gives the right of sharing and not the right of domination, that Arabs love control and holding the initiative, and wants husbands and wives with psychological health who accept the other with faults, and says hundreds of problems come to him from relatives on both sides putting their noses into the family's life. He holds that the happiness of the husband's mother is to see her son master of his house and his wife obedient, and the wife's is that her husband be hers and not his family's, each measuring by her own scale. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:10:00}
- He tells (in sense) that [[person/aisha|Aisha]] felt jealous when she learned of the Prophet's marriage to the beautiful [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]] and that [[person/hafsa|Hafsa]] eased it for her by saying she was old, and that when she saw [[person/safiyya-bint-huyayy|Safiyya]] she said I saw nothing but a Jewess, and infers that jealousy between women is natural and that justice in pleasure and anger is required ([[passage/quran-5-8|al-Ma'ida 8]]), and asks mothers and sisters to intervene only within limits and a mother not to look at her son as the child who was on her shoulder. These are the lecturer's stories and view. (needs_source: the account of Aisha's jealousy of Umm Salama and Safiyya is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:12:30}
- He says God commanded companionship in kindness with the mother ([[passage/quran-31-15|Luqman 15]]) and living with the wife in kindness ([[passage/quran-4-19|al-Nisa 19]]), and that putting them in two pans of a scale is wrong to both since each has a different kind of rights, likening it to comparing pens with towers. He warns the husband that in a moment of closeness he complains of his family to his wife and then things change, and says (in sense) love your beloved moderately and hate your hated moderately. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: the saying 'love your beloved moderately' is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:15:30}
- He asks the husband not to carry his irritability over to his wife, since his father and mother put up with him because he is part of them, but his wife is not part of him, and the wife not to be lazy and sleep till mid-morning with an employed husband nor treat him as a policeman treats a thief, and that obedience is in what is right. He says doing good to the wife is doing good in the face of bad manners, foulness of tongue and stinginess, and that the Prophet took the opinion of [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]] (in sense). This is the lecturer's counsel. (needs_source: the account of the Prophet taking Umm Salama's opinion is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:19:00}
- He gives three counsels: to the relatives of both sides not to put their noses into their sons' and daughters' homes, to the wife's mother to teach her daughter that her loyalty after marriage is to her husband, and to the husband not to stop his wife from honouring her family and keeping ties, even with a weekly visit. He says visit seldom and love grows (a current saying) except for a mother who has only this son ([[passage/quran-17-23|al-Isra 23]]). To the wife he says to count the husband's mother as a mother and bear with her, then please her husband in her, and God will be pleased with her and she will find someone to care for her in old age. See [[practice/birr-al-walidayn|honouring parents]]. This is the lecturer's counsel. (needs_source: the saying 'visit seldom' has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:22:00}
- He says commanding and forbidding with no logic does not work, so it is wrong for a husband to say you will never visit your mother, and whoever wants to be obeyed should command what can be done, homes not being military barracks. He warns against nosiness about what a husband bought and why he bought for his wife and not his sister, and says (his view) that the husband's poor brother and sister are among the first to receive zakat from him while his mother, whom he must maintain, does not receive it ([[concept/zakat|zakat]]), and that souls are disposed to love one who does them good (in sense). He calls for seeking God's help, praying two units, and not carrying tomorrow's worry. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: the ruling on giving zakat to siblings and not the mother has no evidence stated and the saying on disposition has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:26:30}
- Calls: a woman from Libya complains that her husband, forty-five, speaks with other girls and claims one of them he teaches the Qur'an; Su'ad from Germany, divorced for more than ten years, says her former husband's mother died angry with her and with him since he did not reconcile her, and asks whether what befell her of displacement was because of her husband's disobedience to his mother; Umm Zayd says her mother-in-law treats her husband like a child and he puts his mother and sisters before her and says I will not bring you anything before my mother, and asks about obeying her husband if he asks her to take a loan she does not want. Personal details come from text partly garbled. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled and the question on the loan is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:31:00}
- Abd al-Salam from Libya suggests that spouses look at life through the verse on affection and mercy between spouses ([[passage/quran-30-21|al-Rum 21]]) in how each treats the other's mother, and the lecturer thanks him and prays for him. Then Umm Abdullah from al-Ain complains that her husband suddenly changed his mind and wants to return to his own country after they agreed to settle, and a caller from Dubai describes a husband who prays, fasts and has performed the pilgrimage but whose tongue hurts her and who thinks himself better than she, so that she feels she deals with a small boy. No answer is recorded for the last in this passage. (needs_source: no answer is recorded for the caller from Dubai) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:34:00}
- Rashida from Spain says she has been married thirteen years and something befell her in pregnancy or childbearing whose cause she does not know, and asks whether it is a punishment from God, and Umm Fatima from Dubai mentions her daughter who has epilepsy and asks for prayer for healing, and the lecturer prays for her and advises (in an unclear form) the recitation of Surat al-Baqara. The text is garbled and nothing beyond what is clear is recorded. (needs_source: the text of both calls is garbled and the form of the advice is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:36:30}
- To the caller from Libya whose husband speaks with other girls he advises that she pray for him in every prayer that God protect him from the human and jinn devil, that she look at her own shortcomings, and that she take his good qualities and develop and enlarge them, and not call him to account night and day over small and large matters like a school principal with a pupil. This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:37:30}
- To Su'ad he says disobedience to parents is a great calamity, expressing a point about its consequence in wording that is garbled in the text, and that the son should keep his mother's ties and pray for her in every prayer, living or dead, perhaps he may be dutiful to her after her death. Then he appeals to rulers and embassies to care for migrant widows and divorced women and return them to their families. See [[concept/uquq-al-walidayn|disobedience to parents]]. This is the lecturer's answer; the statement on the disobedient one's fate is garbled. (needs_source: the statement on the disobedient one's fate is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:39:30}
- To Umm Muhammad, who complains that her mother-in-law pampers her son and daughters more than she pampers his wife, he says one should look at priorities since a woman who has passed sixty or seventy does not need what a wife of thirty needs, that whoever is disobedient to his mother and father will be disobedient to her, and that what he calls marital flattery, pampering him a little, brings him back to her, and recalls the verse of affection and mercy ([[passage/quran-30-21|al-Rum 21]]). This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:41:30}
- To Umm Abdullah from al-Ain he says the wife should try to persuade her husband to stay if she sees it is better, and if he is not convinced she should follow her husband, and that this should be by understanding and not by commands, prohibitions or unilateral decisions, since the contract gives the right of sharing and not domination. This is the lecturer's view on a disputed question; he gave no evidence. (needs_source: the ruling that the wife follows her husband has no evidence stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:42:30}
- Umm Muhammad from Bahrain says that after twenty-eight years of marriage she was divorced and her children scattered, and she lives with her daughters in Bahrain while her husband and his family do not ask after them, and she has no home or shelter in her own country, and the lecturer tells her that twenty-eight years are not lost with God, that the problem lies with the father and not with her, and prays that she be granted success. Private personal details are mentioned briefly. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:45:30}
- Umm Fatima says she has been married four years and has two girls, that her husband is extremely irritable, insults, and raises his hand to her and the two little ones, and will not agree to see a psychiatrist because he sees the illness in her. The lecturer says this is projection, a person placing his illness on another, and advises her to pray often in prostration that God guide him. He mentions that the 'black box' is the box of painful memories each person carries, which empties its feelings in the house and fills it with gloom, and calls on Islamic states for an urgent fund for widows and divorced women. This is the lecturer's answer and interpretation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:48:30}

## Episode 29: hope in the home
- He asks where hope has gone from the faces of sons, daughters and spouses, and wants to plant it, saying Islam forbids pessimism and loves the good omen. He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] asked army commanders to send him a messenger of fine name and looks, and that a messenger named Nasr came to announce the Muslims' victory at al-Qadisiyya under [[person/saad-ibn-abi-waqqas|Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas]]. See [[event/battle-of-al-qadisiyya|al-Qadisiyya]] and [[concept/tafaul|optimism]]. This is the lecturer's view and story. (needs_source: the story of Umar and the messenger named Nasr has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:03:00}
- He describes the pessimist as seeing the wide as narrow, the many as few and the favour as a scourge, and calls each person to live his day, since past sorrow does not return, and to meet tomorrow with optimism, the dead not being the one who died and rested but the one who lived gloomy. Then to accept reality, saying contentment with reality does not conflict with ambition, and mentions that on the day of Uhud when the Prophet's death was rumoured some Companions sat down, one asking why fight and another saying we fight and die on what he died on, then they learned he was alive and hope returned (in sense). See [[event/battle-of-uhud|Uhud]]. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the account of the Companions at Uhud is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:06:30}
- He calls for making the smile in the heart and not on the lips alone, and for rejoicing in Islam, in the spread of the call and the filling of mosques, and for love as the capital, so that one hates no one and neither harbours rancour nor envies. This is the lecturer's guidance. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:08:00}
- He draws on stories: [[person/kafur-al-ikhshidi|Kafur al-Ikhshidi]], the slave who at a grill seller's said he hoped to rule Egypt and did rule it, [[person/salah-al-din-yusuf-ibn-ayyub|Saladin]], whom his uncle smuggled as an infant in a box and who later expelled the Crusaders, and [[person/qutuz|Qutuz]] and the Tatars who later became Muslim. He mentions [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]] imprisoned nineteen months in a pit thirty-two metres deep with sixty-four steps, and his saying what can my enemies do to me, my garden and orchard are in my chest, if they imprison me my prison is seclusion with God, if they exile me my exile is travel, and if they kill me my death is martyrdom. These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: stories and sayings without a chain or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:12:30}
- He says to one who is dejected today why not be hopeful: today you are not devout so tomorrow you will be, today you were lazy over the Qur'an so tomorrow you will read. He mentions [[person/ata-ibn-abi-rabah|Ata ibn Abi Rabah]], who was one-eyed, flat-nosed, lame and the rest, yet the caliph ordered that none give legal opinions but Ata (his description, without a source), so his defect did not stop his hope, and calls on the children to hope although their parents may hide their worries. This is the lecturer's view and story. (needs_source: the description of Ata ibn Abi Rabah and the ruler's order have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:14:30}
- He says the key to failure is to please everyone you know, and that the friend is the one who brings you closer to God's pleasure and gives you hope. He tells that Thomas Edison was born in Ohio in 1843 and expelled from school after three months for his many questions, whereupon his mother set up a small laboratory for him, and that he obtained 173 patents, invented the phonograph and sold it for forty thousand dollars, then the electric lamp, and wishes he had been a Muslim so that his work would be continuing charity. These are reports without a source. (needs_source: the reports on Edison have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:18:00}
- He tells (in sense) that when [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]] left [[person/hajar|Hajar]] and her son [[person/ismail|Ismail]] in a valley with no crops she did not despair and ran seven times between al-Safa and al-Marwa until God caused water to flow for them, and holds that Ismail's cry was on behalf of every child deprived of father, medicine or schooling, that we must plant hope in our children, and says to the one with a soul that commands evil do not despair, for it may become tranquil. This is the lecturer's story and view. (needs_source: the story of Hajar is in sense and its details have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:20:30}
- He tells that a scientist named Niels at the University of Copenhagen failed physics and then became the only Danish scientist to win the Nobel Prize in physics (his claim). He says the believer is first to hope because he has a Lord, so do not say Lord I have a great worry but worry I have a great Lord, citing the saying of Lut 'or take refuge in a strong support' ([[passage/quran-11-80|Hud 80]]). He tells (in sense) that when they sought a doctor for [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]] he said they sought the doctor of creatures and I sought the doctor of heaven, and that the healer is God. These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: the claim on Niels and the Nobel and the story of al-Shafi'i have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:23:00}
- He tells that in the two years of the caliphate of [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] the zakat collectors returned from Africa with the money because they found no poor man to give it to, and says Africa today is the picture of famine because the rich ate more than their right and some states throw tomatoes and maize into the sea to keep prices up (his claim), and says (in sense) that God placed the poor man's food in the surplus of the rich man's wealth and no poor man goes hungry but through a rich man's surfeit, that if zakat were collected no poor man would remain in the nation, and that the world has become one village in communications and spying but not in giving. This is the lecturer's story and view. (needs_source: the story of the zakat collectors and the claims on zakat, famine and dumping crops have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:25:30}
- He says [[person/abd-al-rahman-al-dakhil|Abd al-Rahman al-Dakhil]] fled the Abbasids to al-Andalus and founded a civilisation that lasted eight centuries (his claim). He calls on a person not to carry worries since worry tightens the chest and to know that all is by God's decree and not to object to His ordaining, pointing to verses on submitting to the decree which are not reproduced, and mentions the difference between a nation whose fourth is their Lord and one whose fourth is their dog ([[passage/quran-58-7|al-Mujadila 7]] and [[passage/quran-18-22|al-Kahf 22]]). See [[place/al-andalus|al-Andalus]]. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the duration of the civilisation of al-Andalus and the story of al-Dakhil have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:29:30}
- Calls: Umm Hadi from al-Ain says her son, twenty, is tense with his father and has not entered the house for months; Habib from the Netherlands asks about airport scanners that show the body's details and whether Muslims in Europe must submit; Iman from Syria says her husband has married another; Walid from Egypt asks on a matter outside the episode and the lecturer refers him to the studio phone; Ahmad from Iraq says his two children lack complete official papers and are threatened with stopping school in Abu Dhabi, their father being ill. Personal details come from text partly garbled. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:34:30}
- More calls: Hiya from Jordan says her brother lost his job in Saudi Arabia because of a phobia and is depressed; a woman from Abu Dhabi with five sisters and one brother whom their mother spoiled says he is now twenty-two and looks down on his mother; a woman from Oman wants to strengthen her certainty; Reem from Algeria left prayer for three years and tries to make up the prayers but fails; Amal from Sharjah says three sisters do not get along; Mahmoud from Egypt is short of patience and irritable; Ahmad from Egypt says a colleague in a niqab is hard to deal with; and Abu Muhammad from Dubai, who left prayer for thirty-seven years and has prayed for ten days, asks whether his prayer is accepted. The text is partly garbled. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:41:00}
- To Abu Muhammad he says that, God willing, God accepts repentance and prayer, and that we have hope in the God who gave us Islam without our asking. To Umm Hadi he says the parents' prayer for their children is among the answered prayers even with a son's disobedience, that our mistakes may be a cause of his rebellion, through wealth with a doubt in it, absence of an example and bad company, so we pray for him and change how we treat him. On the airport scanners he holds it is part of the limits of personal freedom that the nakedness of Muslim men and women not be viewed, and refers the question to the European Council for Fatwa and does not himself give a ruling. This is the lecturer's answer, not a fatwa. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:44:00}
- To Iman, whose husband married another, he says each person has a door of trial and this is God's decree and ordaining, that the second wife's provision with this man was written for her while in her mother's womb, and that she should be content, patient and seek reward, and not waste her good deeds by backbiting the second wife, describing her as not prettier or richer, and should pray that God open her chest. On the Iraqi woman's two children he hopes the minister of education and the officials in Abu Dhabi will consider her request. This is the lecturer's answer on a sensitive matter, his opinion. (needs_source: the statement that the second wife's provision was written has no evidence stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:47:00}
- On Hiya's brother who lost his job through a phobia he says the believer does not know phobia since the believer has an open chest and relies on God, says Hitler feared narrow places because his stepfather shut him in one as a child and he killed himself in the bunker (his claim), and calls for housing the birds of hope in the heart. To the sister from Oman he lists four matters: no rest in this world, no trick in provision, no intercession in death and no turning back of God's decree; to Reem that she make up prayers with each prayer time and stand before dawn; to the three sisters that love builds and hatred demolishes; and to Mahmoud that he pray God to make him among the patient. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]] and [[concept/qadar|the decree]]. This is the lecturer's answer. (needs_source: the claim on Hitler's phobia and suicide has no source and the four-matter saying has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep29 00:51:00}

## Episode 30: the negotiating table in the home
- He says people of all differing ideas sit at a negotiating table, whereas in many Muslim homes the legs of that table are broken and silence has settled so that each is an island apart, and holds that disagreement in homes is natural but the trouble is closed minds and the thought that one alone is right. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:02:30}
- He says the dryness of feeling that husbands and wives complain of is the nature of a world that changes between health and illness, narrowness and ease, and God may wish to stir the heart toward Him, so the servant rises at night and prays earnestly. He counts the first calamity as silence: a man eloquent at his conference and institution who turns mute on entering the house, and a woman who talks for hours with her friends and mother but is at a loss for words when her husband comes, so the husband becomes an unwelcome person. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:06:30}
- He says affection and mercy are a provision from God ([[passage/quran-30-21|al-Rum 21]]) and come only if we lower the eye from one another's faults, mentions the practice of ignoring and forgiving, tells (in sense) that a man said come let us forgive one another and not blame, says the Arabs are not good at blame, and cites a saying he ascribes to [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]], be a fire and not a conflagration, and asks that we not belittle others' feelings. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: stories and sayings given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:08:00}
- He says that when the feeling of one spouse cools Satan tells him this coolness is hatred of him, so the gap widens, and calls for a person to feign dullness as is said that the dullard is not master of his people but their master is the one who feigns dullness, and not to blame over everything since no one is free of fault except the Prophet, and mentions in sense that all the sons of Adam err and that had they not sinned God would bring a people who sin and then ask forgiveness. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: the proverb and the report 'all the sons of Adam err' are given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:11:00}
- He mentions envy between siblings and that the brothers of [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] said Yusuf and his brother are dearer to our father than we are, and we are a group ([[passage/quran-12-8|Yusuf 8]]), and tells that a wise Arab woman was asked who is nearest to your heart and said the small child until he grows, the sick until he recovers and the traveller until he returns (a story without a chain). He says [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]] had great hope that the valley with no crops would turn green, and it turned green with feelings and values, and calls for watering the emotional desert. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the story of the wise Arab woman has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:14:30}
- He mentions what is said of [[person/zakariyya|Zakariyya]], 'and We set right for him his wife' ([[passage/quran-21-90|al-Anbiya 90]]) and explains that the Lord (rabb) is the one who rears, and rearing needs a rearer, one reared and a curriculum, so the rearer is our Lord, the reared are we and the curriculum is that of heaven, and faults the spouse who turns into the advising teacher and the other into the failing pupil, and the woman who attends lectures and then treats her husband as a policeman treats a thief. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:15:00}
- He criticises making the children compete to excel over one another, and says one should not praise one girl's intelligence and beauty before her sister so that she feels lacking, and mentions the proverb love and conceal, and holds that [[person/yaqub|Yaqub]]'s showing his love for Yusuf stirred his sons' jealousy. Then he mentions that when Yusuf's brothers came to him in Egypt they said hardship has touched us and our family, so be charitable to us ([[passage/quran-12-88|Yusuf 88]]), and says glory to Him who exalts by obedience and abases by disobedience, and asks one who sees coolness from a husband to turn to God, since hearts are between two fingers of the Merciful (in sense). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the report that hearts are between two fingers is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:19:00}
- He says the sensitive wife needs patience since her sensitivity comes from her need for tenderness, and asks her not to overdo it since the husband may tire and because sensitivity for twenty years is not of Ayyub or the Prophet. He mentions that the Prophet's households had two parties: the younger under [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and the elder under [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]], who united only to ask him for more maintenance, so he withdrew from them for twenty-nine days and then the choice came down ([[passage/quran-33-28|al-Ahzab 28]] and [[passage/quran-33-29|29]]), and he went in to Aisha and said consult your parents, and she said I choose God and His Messenger and the abode of the hereafter, and asked that he not tell any of his wives what she said. This is the lecturer's account, in sense. (needs_source: the account of the Prophet's withdrawal and Aisha's reply and request is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:23:00}
- He tells (in sense) that [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Ibn Umar]] asked [[person/aisha|Aisha]] about the most wondrous thing she saw of the Prophet, and she said he came in with her under the cover and then said will you let me tonight rise before my Lord, and she said she loved his nearness and preferred his wish, so he rose to pray and wept until the ground was wet with his tears, and said shall I not be a grateful servant. He says this is a refinement of feeling that those about to marry should learn. This is the lecturer's account. (needs_source: the account of Ibn Umar and Aisha is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:25:00}
- He says the contentment lost from hearts was with the grandparents despite few means and that wealth is wealth of the soul, pointing to verses of al-Shafi'i on self-respect which are not reproduced. He mentions that between spouses there are feelings that cannot be described, and tells (in sense) that the Prophet wanted to lengthen a prayer, heard a child crying and shortened it. He gives the example of a wife who loves the kitchen and waits for a word of praise for her food, whom her husband tells that his mother was better, and says this is folly, since souls are disposed to love those who do them good. These are the lecturer's views. (needs_source: the account of the Prophet shortening the prayer is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:28:30}
- Calls: Abu Ibrahim from Britain asks prayer for his mother before an operation and the lecturer prays for her; a woman from Tunisia asks about her sister marrying a Shia and the lecturer refers her to the jurisprudence episodes and does not answer; Masood from Dubai, whose wife complains of a faithless husband who has drunk for six years; Abd al-Aziz from Saudi Arabia, a woman who admits she does not accept criticism; Umm Alaa from Dubai, whose husband flares up quickly and will not open the door of dialogue; and a man who mentions an interest-bearing loan and asks how to be free of it ([[concept/riba|usury]]). The text is partly garbled. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:35:00}
- More calls: Umm Rania from Saudi Arabia asks prayer for her brother and says her husband boycotts his maternal uncles; Umm Ahmad from Ajman, whose husband was made to marry her against the wish of his cousin and whose family ask him to divorce her while he refuses; a woman from Sharjah whose husband is struggling at work and irritable and who also asks about an inheritance; Umm Adnan from Algeria, a divorced woman, who advises the sisters to keep the negotiating table; and Suzan from Ajman, married three years, whose husband flares up and raises his hand. Personal details come from text partly garbled. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled and the inheritance question has no recorded answer) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:41:00}
- To the caller whose husband drinks he says that whoever disobeys God among us, we are bound to obey God in dealing with him, that this is a sick man to be given the medicine gently and kindly and not violently, so we show him affection, absorb his anger, widen the chest and lower the wing, and this may be her door to Paradise, and the best she can give him is prayers in the depth of the night. He warns neighbours and relatives who bear false witness that a suitor is excellent, reminds fathers of the saying that if one comes whose religion and character you approve, marry him (in sense), and advises her to fair patience. See [[concept/khamr|wine]]. This is the lecturer's answer, not a fatwa. (needs_source: the report 'if one comes whose religion you approve' is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:45:00}
- To Abd al-Aziz, who does not accept criticism, he says this is a gap in the heart of many of us, a space of pride, and mentions that religion is sincere advice (in sense) so a person must accept criticism even if it differs from his opinion, repeats the rule accept the truth even from a distant enemy, and mentions that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] used to pray at the start of his rule that God make his strength against the people of falsehood (without a chain). He commends this righteous wife for her admission. See [[concept/nasiha|sincere advice]]. This is the lecturer's answer. (needs_source: the report that religion is sincere advice and the prayer of Umar have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:47:30}
- To Umm Alaa, whose husband flares up, he says to be patient and change the way she presents things, and tells that a king told an interpreter I dreamed my teeth fell out and he said your family will die so the king was angry and punished him, and another said you will be the last of them to die and was rewarded, and holds that she should tell him by allusion of a case at someone else's. To the indebted man he says to say istighfar abundantly and God will make a way out for him and provide for him from where he does not expect. To Umm Rania he says her husband's boycott of his maternal uncles is a great error from his parents and he must not cut his kin, and if she boycotts her brothers she incurs sin. This is the lecturer's answer and story. (needs_source: the story of the king and the interpreter has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep30 00:50:00}

## Episode 31: estrangement in the home
- He asks why some people feel estranged in their homes, as husband, wife, son or daughter, as if playing a string other than the family's, mentions verses on the exile of feelings which are not reproduced, and tells one who sows good and reaps only thorns to sow good and he will reap good. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:03:00}
- He mentions (in sense) the Prophet's saying blessed are the strangers, who are few righteous people among many corrupt, and explains the proverb 'woe to the grieved from the carefree' by a mother whose child is ill and busy night and day while a relative visits for minutes and goes, and by one who grieves at what befalls Muslims in the community while living with a relative who cares only for his team's win. He says the first gap in the wall of estrangement is that the two sides do not reach the shore of trust, so searching a son and daughter in every small and great matter makes them lose trust, and there must be a space of trust between spouses and children. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the report on the strangers and the proverb are given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:07:30}
- He lists the priorities of a husband that make estrangement: first the circle of friends, then work, whose page he should turn on returning, then hobbies such as football and television, mentioning that an hour before the television raises heart disease by eighteen per cent and strains the eye like reading for eight hours (his claim), then his family, then the children in fifth place whom he takes as a cash machine, and last his wife, which makes her feel estranged. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. (needs_source: the figures on heart disease and eye strain from television have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:12:30}
- On the other side some wives begin with the children, then the house and its cleanliness, then a cat or dog or hobbies, then the mirror and cosmetics, and then remember there is a man with whom she has a paper at the registrar's, so the husband becomes a stranger in his home. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:14:00}
- He says (in sense) that the Prophet used to distribute his glances among his companions so that each thought he was the one meant, and this is social intelligence, and that holders of the highest degrees may have social stupidity, giving Einstein, Napoleon and Shakespeare as examples (his claim). He calls on us not to put all the eggs in one basket but to take a share in every door of good: worship, dealings, the Qur'an, exercise, the children and keeping kinship. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the description of the Prophet's glances and the reports on the geniuses have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:15:30}
- He says one who is committed in a home of uncommitted people feels estranged, and that a home where voices rise and each sits before his screen makes its members strangers though they are of one flesh and blood and DNA proves their kinship. He says the grandfather hurried home after work whereas the grandson flees to the cafe and sits three hours with the same group on the same subject, and calls on the wife to ask her children why their father flees the home. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:19:00}
- He says if marriage were turned into a job with a rigid contract its spirit would go, and speaks of the suspended wife who is neither wife nor divorced and stands in court queues for years ([[passage/quran-4-129|al-Nisa 129]]), proposes to Islamic states a fund for such women, and says the judge is human with papers before him and people only God knows are truthful. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep31 00:23:30}
- He asks the husband who accuses his wife of being careless and stupid whether he has set right what is between him and God so that He sets his wife right as for Zakariyya ([[passage/quran-21-90|al-Anbiya 90]]).
